# Cross-workstream dependencies — "WS-xx assumes WS-yy provides Z" statements. Append-only.

| ID | Assumption | Provider | Contract |
|---|---|---|---|

| XD-FIX-R1-113 | **qa schema tenancy.** (1) WS-10 creates role `qa_analytics_owner` exactly like `memory_analytics_owner` (NOLOGIN, BYPASSRLS, member of `r_cross_tenant_read`, no other grants); the qa DDL makes it the owner of `qa.flake_summary_daily` (aggregate across tenants, no test IDs or messages) and grants `grafana_ro` SELECT on that view only. (2) `qa.test_results` (key `observed_at`), `qa.indexed_runs` (key `observed_at` = report `finished_at`) and `qa.test_stats_daily` (key `day_start`, timestamptz at UTC midnight) are monthly RANGE-partitioned and registered in `ops.partitioned_tables` with `key_kind = 'timestamptz'`, `retain_months = 4` (120 days, C-PG). (3) **Partition RLS gap, found by `05-qa/tools/test_qa_rls.sh` on postgres:17:** RLS on a partitioned parent does not apply when a partition is queried directly, and WS-10's default privileges grant `r_<schema>_read` SELECT on every new partition, so a direct `SELECT … FROM qa.test_results_2026_11` returns every tenant. WS-10's partition maintenance (`ops.ensure_partitions` or equivalent) must, for every partition it creates in any schema registered with tenant RLS: `SELECT ops.apply_tenant_rls('<schema>.<partition>')` **and** `REVOKE ALL ON <schema>.<partition> FROM r_<schema>_read, r_<schema>_write`. The same gap exists for every other partitioned tenant table (memory, cost, audit), not only qa. (4) The view `qa.test_stats_30d` is `WITH (security_invoker = true, security_barrier = true)` | WS-10 (all schemas' partition maintenance), WS-04, WS-03, WS-08 (same gap) | 05-qa/sql/qa-schema.sql; 05-qa/tools/test_qa_rls.sh |

### XD-FIX-R1-132 (co-owner WS-07)
Ramps are C-DEF 2.4.0 `ramps[]`. WS-07 adopts exactly: `registry.bundle_assignment_ramps.slot` CHECK `slot BETWEEN 0 AND 2`, non-overlap by constraint trigger `registry.check_ramp_ranges` (WS-06 DDL), API `PUT|DELETE /v1/releases/{env}/assignment/ramps/{slot}` with body `Ramp = {candidate, purpose: canary|holdback|experiment, percent (multipleOf 0.01), bucket_lo (0–10000), tenants_included[], tenants_excluded[], pipeline_id?}` and `Assignment.ramps: Ramp[] (maxItems 3)`; slots 0–1 canaries or experiment, slot 2 holdback at `[9500,10000)`. Remove the singular `ramp`, `AssignmentInput`, and "refuses a second build ramp until V06-14" (architecture.md §2.8); the UI shows up to three ramp rows.

### XD-FIX-R1-136 (co-owner WS-07)
Remove the "0% ramping version" from ui/releases.md and architecture.md. The only precondition shown is "candidate worker build registered on all task queues" (`409 worker_build_not_registered` from `setRamp`).

### XD-FIX-R1-137 (co-owners WS-01, WS-07)
(a) Intake: `AssignmentError` → `503 bundle_unresolvable`, `Retry-After: 30`, **no intake row**; channel adapters retry from their outbox with the same `dedupe_key`; counter `replicant_intake_bundle_unresolvable_total{env,tenant,reason}`. WS-01 never falls back to `default` (a fallback drops tenant layers). (b) Pause semantics: `release.hold` freezes stage progression only, traffic unchanged; stopping candidate traffic = set that ramp's `percent` to 0. There is no `paused` column; WS-01 versioning §5 "Pause ramp" means `release.hold`. (c) Ramp guards are the C-DEF §4.4 table (canary and experiment → `canary`; holdback → `promoted|deprecated`), plus CCR-06-12 rows. (d) Single retire guard for a `replicant-worker` build: no default, rollback target, ramp candidate (any purpose), tenant override, bundle in `shadowing|canary`, or running pinned workflow references it (release-bundle.md §10). WS-01 uses this text verbatim.

WS-04 exposes memory-service component endpoints callable by `svc_eval-worker` for the seven suites of eval-harness.md §11 (`memory_extraction`, `memory_grounding`, `memory_dedupe`, `memory_retrieval`, `memory_capacity_curve`, `memory_safety_classifier`, `memory_matcher`). WS-10 DR step 10 calls `POST /v1/admin/evals/resync?since=<restore point>`. WS-03 marks `codex.mode: app_server` and `cli: claude-agent-sdk` as eval-only capabilities; the bundle build sets `eval_only` from them.

### XD-FIX-R1-158 (co-owner WS-07)
`intake.job_requests.assignment_reason` CHECK = `^(default|eval_pin|shadow_pin|explicit_pin|ramp:(canary|holdback|experiment):[0-2]|tenant_override:(holdback_tenant|pinned_by_operator|layer_pending))(\+layer)?$`. New column `intake.job_requests.release_cohort text NOT NULL CHECK (release_cohort IN ('default','candidate','holdback','experiment','override','eval','shadow'))`, set by intake from the reason (release-bundle.md §8.3). New column `jobs_read.jobs.migrated boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false`, set by the projector on timeline event `bundle.migrated` (and then the job's metrics carry `release_cohort="migrated"`).

### XD-FIX-R1-134 (co-owners WS-01, WS-02, WS-09, WS-10, WS-03)
WS-01: ReplicantJob with `options.evaluation.harness_task` skips router and graph and runs node `harness_task` (NodeCatalog role `auxiliary`), which for each step waits up to 15 min for `inputs/manifest.json` at the precomputed run prefix, then calls the normal Runner-call activity (grant signed in core) with `role: eval`; the job ends `succeeded` when all steps ran, `failed` only on infrastructure errors. WS-02: runner-api admits global `replicant.example.com/eval-toolchain: "true"` AgentDefinitions outside the closure only for `role: eval` in tenant `replicant-eval` (CCR-06-07); repo-mirror serves **production** hosts to `replicant-eval` tokens only for runs with network profile `eval-snapshot` and role `eval` (fixes the contradiction between fast-path.md §4.3 and network.md). WS-09/WS-10: eval-worker gets `s3:PutObject` on `arn:aws:s3:::<org>-replicant-<env>-artifacts/tenants/replicant-eval/jobs/*/runs/*/inputs/*` and `s3:GetObject` on `…/tenants/replicant-eval/jobs/*` (reading results), with the `replicant-eval` tenant KMS key; no `kms:Sign` on the run-grant key. WS-03: a gateway-stub (scripted mode) Deployment in namespace `replicant-eval` for compatibility checks C1–C4 and C6–C9.

### XD-FIX-R1-133 (co-owner WS-07)
Intake accepts a shadow JobRequest when the requester is `{kind: service, principal_id: svc_release-controller, on_behalf_of: <J.requester>}`, `options.evaluation = {mode: shadow, shadow_of_job_id, pipeline_id}`, and `requested_release_bundle_id` is in state `shadowing` and is the candidate or its derived bundle for the job's tenant (CCR-06-12). `svc_release-controller` holds `release:pin` and `eval:run`. The D-18 repository check uses `on_behalf_of`. Such jobs are registered with budget account `fgw:pipeline:<pipeline_id>` and do not count against the tenant's quota.

### XD-FIX-R1-285 (co-owner WS-10)
WS-10's role matrix gives `svc_eval_worker` SELECT on `jobs_read`, `intake.job_requests`, `approvals`, `cost` (CCR-06-15), in addition to `{ops, tenancy, registry}`.

### XD-FIX-R2-021 (co-owners WS-07, WS-01)
WS-01: on `release.notice(bundle_rolled_back|bundle_revoked)` mark open `plan_approval`/`publish_approval` gates `release_block` (GateView field, CCR-06-13), project, and reject `approve` without `override_release_block` (`GateBlockedByRelease`); `job.offer_bundle_migration(consent=operator)` migrates at the next safe point without a gate; undecided `required` migrations end `cancelled` with reason `bundle_revoked_not_migrated` at the deadline (no `expired`). WS-07: the rollback dialog calls `GET /v1/releases/{env}/assignment:rollback-preview`, shows blast radius, sends `preview_digest`, has no default except `required_by_operator` preselected for a failed canary; the approval packet and all channels show the banner text of promotion.md §7.3; the override needs a typed reason and is audited.

### XD-FIX-R2-056 (co-owner WS-02)
WS-06 adopts WS-02's PriorityClass `replicant-eval` (value 50, `preemptionPolicy: Never`). The eval tenant's Runner quota is 90 concurrent runs and 2,000 runs per day (tenancy operational setting; satisfies R2-055's required tenant quota for `replicant-eval`).

### XD-FIX-R2-061 (co-owners WS-03, WS-07)
WS-03 gateway: budget accounts keyed `fgw:eval:<eval_run_id>`, `fgw:pipeline:<pipeline_id>`, `fgw:proposal:<prop_id>`, each with a USD cap set at registration; API `PUT /internal/v1/budgets/jobs/{job_id} {account, cap_usd}` callable by control-plane-api; spend on such jobs is not charged to the tenant quota; usage events and `cost` rollups gain columns `evaluation_mode` (`eval|shadow|none`) and `budget_account`. WS-07 intake registers every eval, harness, shadow and proposer job with its account in the intake transaction's after-commit hook (retried until done before the workflow's first run).

### XD-FIX-R2-063 (co-owner WS-03)
Add `eval-judge` to the gateway role enum and to the `cost` usage-role CHECK; accept eval-worker as a service caller (also XD-FIX-R1-150). Judge calls carry `X-Replicant-Role: eval-judge`, `X-Replicant-Job-Id` of the case's job, and budget account `fgw:eval:<eval_run_id>`.

### XD-FIX-XD-ORCH-01 (co-owners WS-03, WS-01, WS-07)
WS-06 defines the expected assignment window (60 days from first build; derived bundles inherit the base's end; rebuilds of existing content skip the check; release-bundle.md §3.4) and the calendar `registry.model_retirements` with `GET/PUT /v1/releases/model-retirements[/{id}]`. WS-03's gateway model catalog exposes `retires_at` per model so `svc_release-controller` can import it daily. WS-01 handles notice kind `model_retiring` (C-MSG wave 0): record, banner, and at its `deadline` accept `job.offer_bundle_migration(required=true, consent=operator)`; ineligible jobs are cancelled with reason `model_retired`. WS-07 shows the calendar on the releases screen.

## XD-ORCH-04: partition-level RLS (orchestrator, 2026-10-24, from WS-05 finding OQ-05-19)

RLS on a partitioned parent does not apply to direct reads of a partition. WS-05 reproduced this on postgres:17: with
WS-10's default privileges, a direct `SELECT` on a partition returned every tenant's rows.

Required fixes:
- **WS-10 (lead):**
  - Partition maintenance applies `ops.apply_tenant_rls` (ENABLE and FORCE) to every new partition and revokes direct
    partition grants from service roles.
  - Default privileges must not grant on partitions.
  - The validator gains a check that queries a partition directly as each service role and expects zero cross-tenant rows.
- **Every owner of partitioned tenant tables (WS-03 cost, WS-04 memory, WS-06, WS-07, WS-08 audit):** conform in
  wave 2 and run the WS-10 check.

The projector inserts every item with `ON CONFLICT (job_id, seq) DO NOTHING RETURNING seq`, emits `audit.emit` for every **newly inserted** gate item (`gate.opened`, `gate.decided`, `gate.decision_superseded`, `gate.decision_denied`, `gate.expired`, `gate.escalated`) in the same transaction **even when the snapshot is stale** (`jobs.seq ≥ snapshot.seq`), and applies the snapshot only under `WHERE jobs.seq < snapshot.seq`. ## WS-01 — 2026-10-24T18:00Z — fix round wave 1: XD-FIX resolutions for seams WS-01 leads (co-owners conform in wave 2)

| ID | Resolution (exact shape co-owners adopt) | Co-owners | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| XD-FIX-R2-010 | **Pause, don't fail.** When an activity's retries run out (`retry_state` MAXIMUM_ATTEMPTS_REACHED or TIMEOUT), the ReplicantJob holds: status `paused`, phase unchanged, timeline `job.blocked_on_dependency`. It re-probes the same activity ID with backoff 60 s doubling to 900 s plus 0–120 s jitter (`repair_limits.dependency_block`), and gives up after `max_hours` = 72 with outcome `dependency_unavailable` (status `failed`, compensation, terminal projection). The execution never fails. `job.resume` during a hold means "retry now". WS-10 `recovery.md` replaces "in-flight jobs pause for the outage, then resume" with exactly this behaviour and states the 72 h limit. WS-08 alerts on `replicant_jobs_held{kind="dependency"}`, derived from the projection `status = paused` plus the latest `job.blocked_on_dependency` event | WS-10, WS-08 | 01 graphs/README.md §2.2 |
| XD-FIX-R2-011 | `gate.decide` never fails after validation. On an identity outage the result is `outcome: denied`, `denial_reason: identity_unverifiable`, and the `decision_id` is **not** recorded. control-plane-api's outbox then re-submits automatically after 60 s, up to 5 times, with a new `decision_id = derived("dec", <original decision_id>, "r<n>")` (n = 1..5) used as the Update ID, and the same `presented_digest`, `actor`, `channel`, `comment` and the **original** `decided_at`. The UI shows "Identity service unavailable, retrying (n of 5)". After 5 retries it shows "Could not verify your identity; try again". Adapters (Slack, GitHub) use the same rule | WS-07 | 01 gates.md §4 |
| XD-FIX-R2-012 | Circuit breaker. WS-03 implements activity `platform.dependency_health` (task queue `replicant-jobs`, implementation in the replicant-worker image, WS-03-owned module `replicant_gateway_client.health`). Input `{dependency ∈ ai-gateway \| repo-mirror \| exec-node-pool, tenant_id}`. Output `{dependency, state ∈ closed \| half_open \| open, since?, reason?}`. `ai-gateway`: the shim's `/readyz` plus the enterprise gateway error rate over 60 s (open when ≥ 50 % of upstream calls fail or `/readyz` fails 3 times in a row; half-open after 120 s). `repo-mirror`: WS-02/WS-09 repo-mirror `/healthz`. `exec-node-pool`: open while the WS-02 controller reports a node-pool disruption (`replicant_exec_nodes_not_ready_ratio > 0.2`). The workflow treats `GatewayUnavailable`, `RepoFetchFailed`, and `NodeLost` while the breaker is open as platform failures. They do not consume `run_retries`; the run restarts after the breaker closes, plus a per-job jitter. WS-03's agentd keeps `GatewayUnavailable` after `unavailableAfterSeconds` (600 s) as the run failure reason | WS-03, WS-02 | 01 graphs/README.md §2.1 |
| XD-FIX-R2-013 | Approval records. ReplicantJob awaits `gates.write_approval_record` and `notify.gate_resolved` (and every `gate.*`, `bundle.*` and `job.terminal` projection) before Continue-As-New or close, with no cap. As a backstop, WS-08 adds an **approval-record reconciler** to `audit-exporter`. For each closed run's skeleton, every `gate.decide` Update whose result was `accepted` must have `audit` `tenants/<t>/jobs/<j>/gates/<gate_id>/record-<decision_id>.json`. A missing one is written from the full export with `If-None-Match: *` (idempotent), and `audit.emit(type="approval_record.reconciled")` is called | WS-08 | 01 gates.md §4 |
| XD-FIX-R2-014 | `GateDecideRequest.decided_at` = control-plane-api's receipt time of the decision (UTC, from its own NTP clock), stamped when it writes the outbox row and **kept unchanged** on every re-submission. Browser, Slack and GitHub timestamps are never used. github-integration and slack-integration forward their own receipt time, and control-plane-api uses the earlier of that and its own. The workflow accepts a decision after `expires_at` only if `decided_at < expires_at`, it arrives within 6 h, and it falls within the 15-minute grace window opened by a late expiry timer (CCR-01-07) | WS-07 | 01 gates.md §4 |
| XD-FIX-R2-019 | `context.stage_inputs` (WS-04) accepts input `approver_feedback: [{gate_id, gate_kind, decision_id, cycle, text (≤ 8000)}]`. It stages each item in the inputs manifest as a block `<untrusted_input source="approver_feedback" gate_kind="…" decision_id="…">…</untrusted_input>` (the same framing as other untrusted inputs) for the next producing node (plan, implement or repair), and returns `approver_feedback_consumed: [decision_id…]`. WS-07's packet revision n+1 shows a section "Addressed feedback from r(n)" listing the comment (rendered from `approval_text`) and the run that addressed it. The dangling reference to XD-01-07 in gates.md is removed | WS-04, WS-07 | 01 gates.md §4 step 7 |
| XD-FIX-R2-023 | Gates opened while the job is paused `at_next_gate` are held: no notifications, no reminders, **no expiry**. On resume, `expires_at += held duration` and timeline `gate.unheld`. The pauser receives `notify.job_event{kind: gate_held, gate_id, gate_kind, to: <principal>, note}`. WS-07 renders it ("Your pause is holding the plan approval for job … Its expiry clock is stopped until you resume"), and the pause popover states "Gates opened while paused are held and do not expire" | WS-07 | 01 gates.md §3 |
| XD-FIX-R2-044 | `repair_limits.job_max_age_days` = 60 (hard job lifetime from creation; outcome `job_max_age_exceeded`, status `expired`). WS-06 sizes prompt escrow (RB-3) and worker/CLI image retention (UP-6) to **67 days** (`job_max_age_days + 7`), replacing 37. Model retirement: WS-06 adds an operator-approved `model_successor` mapping `{retiring_model, successor_model, approved_by, approved_at}`. The registry derives successor bundle B′ for each live bundle using the retiring model (only agent-definition `model` fields and the `models` index differ; `lineage.parent_release_bundle_id = B`). control-plane-api sends `release.notice(model_retiring, recommended_release_bundle_id = B′, deadline = retirement date)`. `release.check_migration_eligibility` (WS-01 activity, registry-backed) gains input `lineage ∈ any \| patch \| model_successor` and returns `eligible=false, reason="lineage_mismatch"` when B′'s diff from B exceeds what that lineage allows | WS-06 | 01 versioning.md §6.1 rules 6–8 |
| XD-FIX-R2-062 | WS-03 implements activity `cost.batch_spend` (task queue `replicant-jobs`). Input `{batch_job_id, tenant_id}`, output `{spent_usd}`: the sum of the cost ledger over all jobs whose `parent_job_id = batch_job_id`. `cost.record_run` input already carries `parent_job_id` (from ReplicantJob state), and WS-03 stores it in the cost ledger row (new nullable column `parent_job_id text`, indexed). The batch reads it before each wave of child starts and stops at `repair_limits.maintenance.max_batch_usd` (default 2000) | WS-03 | 01 graphs/maintenance-fanout.md |

| XD-FIX-R1-013 | WS-06 adds to `registry-releases.sql`: `registry.worker_deployment_versions (deployment_name text not null default 'replicant-worker', build_id text not null check (build_id ~ '^b[0-9]{8}-[0-9a-f]{12}$'), image_digest text not null check (image_digest ~ '^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$'), node_catalog_digest text not null, registered_at timestamptz not null default now(), ready_at timestamptz, retire_after timestamptz, retired_at timestamptz, patch_of_build_id text, incident_id text, primary key (deployment_name, build_id))`.   `memory-injection.json`, which purges are allowed to delete.
  - WS-03 adds the manifest place `system_append_memory` to `replicant.run-inputs/v1`.
  - WS-03's entrypoint joins `system-append.md` and the memory block into the final prompt file on tmpfs (and the Codex
    `AGENTS.md`). That joined file is never uploaded.
- **XD-W2-04-02: accepted.** There are two denial layers:
  - control-plane-api emits `authz.denied`;
  - memory-service emits `memory.admin_denied`.

  Both carry `details.permission = "memory:admin"`. WS-11's probe accepts either; WS-08 seeds both.

## Orchestrator ruling on XD-W2-05-01 (2026-10-24)

The contract wins. In the frozen run-result schema, `outcome: blocked` means "needs a human (clarification)" and nothing else.
Inconclusive QA results map to `outcome: failed`, with `verdict: inconclusive` carried in `role_result`. WS-03 changes its three
rows. XD-W2-05-02..05 are routed to WS-08, WS-02, WS-03 and WS-01 respectively, as written.

## Orchestrator rulings on WS-08 wave-2 corrections (2026-10-24)

1. `ReplicantReleaseFailureSpike`: WS-08's `> on (env) group_left ()` form is **accepted**. A promtool test proves it. WS-07 cites
   the loaded rule.
2. Approval-record reconciler: the contract key `gates/<gate_id>/decision-<decision_id>.json` **wins**. The record is built from
   the `gate.decided` audit row plus `approvals.gate_decisions`. XD-W2-08-03 IAM goes to WS-10, and the key name goes to WS-01.
3. Local container label: the earlier ruling stands, and container names are the only source. WS-08 removes the
   label-when-present branch from `local.alloy`.
4. Metric `replicant_jobs_post_merge_reverted_total` (C-IDS §11 prefix): **accepted**. WS-04 and WS-06 use that name.
5. XD-W2-08-04: legal-hold escalation notifies Okta group `replicant-legal`. WS-09 defines the group and WS-07 notifies it.

### WS-04 wave-2 follow-ups (WS-04, 2026-10-24)
- XD-W2-04-05 (WS-04 -> WS-06, WS-10; answers XD-W2-06-04): The metric is named `replicant_jobs_post_merge_reverted_total{tenant, release_cohort, bundle}`. This replaces the name `replicant_job_post_merge_reverted_total` used in XD-W2-04-03. `release_cohort` uses the WS-06 cohort enum. `bundle` is the job's base bundle short ID. POST /outcomes (OutcomeRequest) now carries `release_cohort` and `merged_at`. Defined in spec/04-memory-and-context/write-path.md §9.
- XD-W2-04-06 (WS-04 -> WS-10, WS-06; answers XD-W2-06-01): `memory.v_analytics_outcomes` holds only aggregates (per tenant, bundle and day), so it cannot back per-job audited reads. WS-04 added the per-job view `memory.v_job_outcomes`, owned by memory_analytics_owner. Its columns are `job_id`, `tenant_id`, `release_bundle_id`, `workflow_type`, `terminal_status`, `pr_merged`, `merged_at`, `post_merge_reverted`, `outcome_report_count`, `review_rounds`, `first_pass_qa`, `first_review_approved`, `report_kind` and `reported_at`. It contains no free text: report notes and evidence URLs are excluded. `eval_metrics_reader` has column-level SELECT on these columns, for `ops.eval_job_outcomes`. `ops.eval_job_memory_usage` reads the jobs_read injection and usage facts and needs nothing new from WS-04. `memory.job_outcomes.merged_at` is a new column.

## Orchestrator rulings on WS-10 wave-2 items (2026-10-24)

- **XD-W2-10-03 (scope for audit-exporter):** neither `org:*` nor `operator:all-tenants` is right. `audit-exporter` is a
  service, so it uses **`system:all-tenants`**. WS-10 and WS-08 align their policies to it.
- **`eval_job_outcomes` scope deviation:** **not accepted as permanent.** WS-04 makes `memory.v_job_outcomes`
  `security_invoker` (XD-W2-10-07), and then all eight `ops.eval_*` functions pin `system:all-tenants`, per the CCR-06-15
  ruling.
- **XD-W2-10-05 (`bundle-ref-` tag body):** **WS-06's per-bundle form wins**, `bundle-ref-<first 20 chars of rb_ body>`.
  ECR tags are unique per repository, a bundle references one image per repository, and an image shared by several
  bundles simply carries several tags. Lifecycle keeps any image with at least one `bundle-ref-` tag.
- **XD-W2-10-06 / CCR-10-12 (`replicant-db-migrate` Temporal identity, `CountWorkflowExecutions` only):** **accepted.**
  WS-00 adds it to C-MSG §1, WS-09 adds it to the Authorizer, and WS-01 notes it.
- **XD-W2-10-01:** WS-08 adds the audit event type `data.cross_tenant_read`.
- **XD-W2-10-04:** WS-06 confirms online-metrics §4 uses only the `ops.eval_*` functions.

## Orchestrator ruling on XD-W2-08-05 (2026-10-24): no tenant-key decrypt for audit-exporter

XD-W2-08-05 is **withdrawn**. Its approach widens `audit-exporter` to decrypt tenant content just to compute a hash, which is
the wrong trade-off. Instead, the writer records the digests at write time.

- **WS-07:**
  - Add `text_sha256` to `approvals.gate_decisions`. control-plane-api computes it when it writes `approval_text`.
  - Add `memory_refs text[]` (`mem_…@v`) to `approvals.gates`, per packet revision. It is filled when the packet is built.
- **WS-08:** the reconciler reads both values from Postgres, through `svc_audit_exporter`'s existing `approvals` read. It reads
  nothing from S3 `artifacts`. The original digest survives a leak purge because it lives in the row.

The XD-W2-08-03 permissions stand (write-once on `gates/*/decision-*.json`). No new KMS grant is needed.

WS-08 2026-10-24T19:25Z: XD-W2-08-05 is withdrawn (orchestrator ruling). The reconciler reads `approvals.gate_decisions.text_sha256` and `approvals.gates.memory_refs` from Postgres; audit-exporter needs no S3 read on `approval_text` or packets and no tenant-key Decrypt.
- XD-W2-04-08 (WS-04 -> WS-01; applies XD-W2-01-05, 2026-10-24): The input of `steering.stage_backlog` is now `{job_id, node_key, cycle, ordinal, message_ids[], messages[]}`. Each `messages[]` item is `{message_id, text (<= 16000, untrusted), author (usr_ principal ID), sent_at}`. `messages[].message_id` must equal `message_ids[]` in order. On a mismatch the activity fails non-retryably with `steering_backlog_mismatch`. The result is `{keys[]}`. Schema: spec/04-memory-and-context/schemas/steering-stage-backlog.schema.json (`#/$defs/result` for the result). Example: examples/steering-stage-backlog.example.json. Python types: models/replicant_context/working_memory.py (SteeringBacklogInput, SteeringMessage, SteeringBacklogResult). memory-service gets no HTTP endpoint for this: the replicant-worker activity writes to S3 directly.

| XD-W3-R1V-006 | **10x repository scale (R2-046):**<br>(1) C-JOBREQ 1.4.2 `target_selector.max_repos` maximum is **15,000** (was 1,500); explicit `targets` stays at 200.<br>(2) A `MaintenanceBatch` resolves its selector into an S3 repo list (`children_index_uri`), never into history. At ≤ 2,000 repos it fans out child jobs itself. Above 2,000 it starts internal children of type **`MaintenanceShard`**: ID `<job_id>:shard:<n>`, task queue `replicant-jobs`, inheriting the batch's pinned version, `ParentClosePolicy.REQUEST_CANCEL`, ≤ 2,000 repos each. Each shard creates its child jobs through `intake.create_child_job` (`ParentClosePolicy.ABANDON`), receives their `batch.child_progress` Signals, enforces its share of the batch concurrency, runs Continue-As-New on its own thresholds, and completes when every child is in `pr_review` or terminal, returning per-status counts. The batch aggregates shard results into `batch.snapshot`.<br>(3) C-DEF `ref_list` `maxItems` stays 500: refs scale with definitions, not repositories.<br>(4) The C-ARCH 1.5.4 §4.1 table records the 10x value and change path for each frozen limit. | WS-01: `MaintenanceShard` workflow, history sizing in maintenance-fanout.md. | XD-W3-R1V-063 (WS-10 is first-listed, so this decides it; supersedes WS-09's 944-line row) | **No `replicant-<env>-s3-batch-offboarding` role**, as in WS-10's earlier XD-W3-R1V-063 row. S3 Batch Operations cannot delete objects or versions: its operation list is copy, checksum, delete-all-tags, invoke Lambda, replace tags, ACL, restore, encryption update, Batch Replication, Object Lock retention, Object Lock legal hold [verified: https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/batch-ops-operations.html, 2026-10-24]. A Batch role with `DeleteObjectVersion` would never be used by a Batch job, and a Lambda-based design would add a third deleting principal for no gain. The offboarding pipeline deletes as `replicant-<env>-tenant-offboarding` (`ListObjectVersions` + versioned `DeleteObjects`, primary and DR). **WS-09:** remove `replicant-<env>-s3-batch-offboarding` from `s3-bucket-policy.artifacts.json`'s deleter list. The list is then `tenant-offboarding`, `leak-purge`, `memory-service`, matching WS-10's generated policies | WS-09 | config/iam/tenant-offboarding.json; lifecycle.md §4 |

## Orchestrator ruling on R1V-023 (2026-10-24, final): QA harness interface

WS-03 and WS-05 each adopted the other's earlier proposal in wave 3. This ruling fixes the interface. WS-03 is
first-listed, so its current `cli-invocation.md` §4.10 is canonical. Neither side changes it again.

- **Command:** `/opt/replicant/bin/replicant-qa run --inputs /replicant/inputs --out /replicant/out/qa --plan /replicant/inputs/run-plan.json`.
  The harness reads `/replicant/inputs/qa/config.json` itself. There is no `--config` and no `--spool`.
- **Checkpoints:**
  - The harness writes to `/replicant/out/qa-checkpoints/<stage>.json`.
  - Retry inputs arrive at `/replicant/inputs/qa/checkpoints/<stage>.json`.
- **Exit classes, used in `result.json.exit_class`, `harness_result.exit_class` and `agentrun_exit_class`:**

  | Exit class | Entrypoint exit |
  |---|---|
  | `pass`, `pass_with_warnings`, `fail`, `inconclusive_other`, `not_applicable` | 0 |
  | `environment_setup` | 44 |
  | `environment_services` | 45 |

  `ok`, `environment_setup_install` and `environment_setup_services` are withdrawn.

WS-05 conforms its schemas, examples, docs and `ClassifyOutput`/`classification_facts` to this.

### WS-04 wave-3 entries (WS-04, 2026-10-24)
- XD-W3-R1V-026 (WS-04 -> WS-01): **`memory.create_snapshot` with mode `revocation_filter`.**
  - Required: `{tenant_id, job_id, mode, base_snapshot_id}`.
  - Filled by memory-service from the job's latest binding (`memory.job_snapshots`) and the base snapshot's pinned policy, so WS-01 does not send them: policy, repos, workflow_type, release_bundle_id, requester_principal_id, scope and evaluation_mode. If any of them is sent, it must equal the bound value, or the call returns 409 `binding_mismatch`.
  - Optional for every mode except `eval_prepare`: `run_id`, `role`, `node_key`, `cycle`, `parent_job_id`. They feed log and trace only.
  - WS-01's current call `{**base, mode, base_snapshot_id}` validates as is (checked against CreateSnapshotRequest), so WS-01 needs no change.
  - The other modes are unchanged: `create`, `eval_pinned`, `policy_refresh` and `bundle_migration` require job_id, repos, workflow_type, release_bundle_id, requester_principal_id and policy. `eval_prepare` requires scope_context and policy.
- XD-W3-R1V-027 (WS-04 -> WS-07, WS-11): **Permission for `restore-resync` and its status poll.** Both layers check `restore:execute`, never `memory:admin`.
  - memory-service's second layer calls identity-service `authorize {action: "restore:execute", tenant_id: null}`. A denial emits `memory.admin_denied` with `details.permission: restore:execute`.
  - WS-07 already declares `x-replicant-permission: restore:execute`. Its description text on these two routes still says `memory:admin` and should be corrected.
  - WS-11's `memory:admin` probe must not list these two routes.
- XD-W3-R1V-029 (WS-04 -> WS-10, WS-07): **Request shape for `POST /v1/admin/memory/restore-resync`.** It follows the XD-FIX-R1-237 (c) envelope.
  - No body. Query `since` (RFC 3339, the restore point) is required. Query `dry_run` (boolean, default false) is optional. RestoreStep echoes `dry_run`.
  - Runbook step 7 becomes `POST /v1/admin/memory/restore-resync?since=<restore point>&dry_run=true`, then the same call with `dry_run=false`, each with its own Idempotency-Key. This supersedes the `{restored_to, dry_run}` body in XD-FIX-R1-102.
  - WS-07's proxy adds the optional `dry_run` query parameter.
- XD-W3-R1V-030 (WS-04 -> WS-02): **Exec revocation feed settings.** memory-mcp and context-mcp use the agreed names: `REPLICANT_EXEC_REVOCATIONS_URLS` (comma list), `REPLICANT_EXEC_REVOCATIONS_TOKEN_FILE` and `REPLICANT_EXEC_REVOCATIONS_POLL_SECONDS` (default 10). The name `REPLICANT_RUNNER_REVOCATIONS_URL` is retired.
- WS-04 side of R1V-008 (WS-01 decides): `context.stage_inputs` takes `qa_resume: {previous_run_id}`.
  - It copies each `qa_report/checkpoints/<stage>.json` from the previous run to `inputs/qa-checkpoints/<stage>.json` and lists it with `place: qa_file`, `path: checkpoints/<stage>.json`. prepare places it at `/replicant/inputs/qa/checkpoints/<stage>.json`.
  - WS-01's graphs/README.md still names `inputs/qa/resume/checkpoints.json`.
- WS-04 side of R1V-064 (WS-10 decides): memory purges delete object versions in the primary bucket and in its `-us-west-2` replica. The deletion-ledger `targets` list both. WS-10's IAM for memory-service needs the matching replica statements.
